* Observe Kimizuka's boredom – he is restless, his screen is broken.
* Recognize this as an opportunity to create a small, intimate moment.
* Offer the earbud – a simple gesture that carries symbolic weight.
* Acknowledge the cultural reference: "Like that classic scene—sharing earphones listening to music in the corner of the classroom."
* When Nagisa corrects her about modern wireless etiquette, she doesn't get defensive – she accepts it playfully.

Conclusion: Siesta uses a small gesture to create intimacy and shared experience, compensating for the normal school life they never had.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Kimizuka: "I'm bored. Pet me."

Nagisa: "Ugh, it's Kimihiko-the-Kid time!?"

Kimizuka: "Why don't you watch movies too?"

Kimizuka: "My screen's busted or something."

Siesta (placing earbud in his ear): "Like that classic scene—sharing earphones listening to music in the corner of the classroom. We never really had that kind of school life, I guess."

Kimizuka: "…Yeah. That's true."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Emotional Intelligence: A - Recognizes Kimizuka's boredom as an opportunity for connection.
Social Awareness: A - Understands the cultural significance of sharing earphones.
Non‑verbal Communication: B - Uses a simple gesture to create intimacy.
Nostalgia Management: A - Compensates for lost school experiences.

Feat 8: Strategic Networking – Meeting the Former Swordmaster

Siesta reveals she met with the former Swordmaster (a retired Tuner) to request cooperation. She explains that due to Alicia's repeated Tuner Hunts, their members have been severely diminished, and they need all the help they can get.

3. Former Swordmaster Premise: There is a retired Tuner with the title "Swordmaster" who can help.
4. Siesta's Initiative Premise: She has already reached out to him.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Identify potential allies – retired Tuners like the Swordmaster.
* Reach out to the Swordmaster and request his cooperation.
* Secure a promise of help "from behind the scenes."

EVENT 4: The Plane Hijacking – Confronting Bat of Another Eden (Chapter 1: Invader from Another Dimension)

Context: During the flight, a door from Another Eden opens, and an alternate version of Bat (from Volume 2) appears, hijacking the plane with tentacles. He demands the detectives. Siesta engages him in combat while Kimizuka evacuates passengers.

Feat 9: Psychological Warfare – Provoking Bat to Reveal His True Orders

When Bat knows about the weapon but not their identities, Siesta provokes him: "You knew about the weapon—but not who we actually are? … Maybe your superior doesn't actually expect much from you." This forces Bat to reveal that his orders were to capture the detectives alive – not kill them.

Premises:

1. Bat's Knowledge Gap Premise: He knows about the musket but doesn't recognize Siesta or Nagisa as detectives.
2. Contradiction Premise: If he was well‑briefed, he would know who they are.
3. Inferiority Premise: Bat is the type to take orders from a superior.
4. Emotional Trigger Premise: Questioning his competence will provoke him to reveal more.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Observe that Bat knows about the weapon but not their identities.
* Identify this as a weakness – his briefing was incomplete.
* Taunt him to provoke a defensive reaction: "Maybe your superior doesn't actually expect much from you."
* When he responds with "Shut up! I just didn't bother to actually care," she notes that he's defensive.
* Continue the engagement to see what else he reveals.
* When Nagisa later uses Word Soul to force him to speak, Siesta's earlier provocation already primed him to confess.

Conclusion: Siesta's taunt is a calculated move to gather intelligence and psychologically destabilize the enemy.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Siesta (sharp): "You knew about the weapon—but not who we actually are? Maybe your superior doesn't actually expect much from you."

Bat: "Shut up! I just didn't bother to actually care. Anyhow, I'm the one calling the shot!"

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Psychological Warfare: A - Uses taunting to provoke emotional response and reveal information.
Behavioral Observation: A - Notes Bat's defensiveness.
Strategic Probing: A - Gathers intelligence through provocation.
Emotional Manipulation: B - Exploits Bat's ego and insecurity.

Feat 10: Combat Analysis – Understanding Bat's Transformation and Identifying the Puppet Master

As she fights Bat, Siesta notices that he is not the same as the Bat they knew – he's from Another Eden. She also uses her observation that Bat is following orders to connect him to Alicia.

Premises:

1. Combat Observation Premise: Bat's behavior is different from the Bat they knew; he is more aggressive and less personal.
2. Order‑Following Premise: Bat is clearly following orders from a superior.
3. Alicia's Involvement Premise: Since this Bat is from Another Eden, the one who sent him must be Alicia.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Fight Bat and observe his style – it's familiar but different.
* Recognize that he's from Another Eden based on his lack of recognition.
* Note that he's following orders, not acting independently.
* Connect to Alicia – she is the Queen of Another Eden.
* State that his orders were "to bring me and Nagisa back—alive. By your pink‑haired queen."

Conclusion: Siesta deduces that Alicia sent Bat, and that the real mission is capture, not kill.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Siesta (certain): "You can't kill the detective. That was never part of the deal. You were ordered to bring me and Nagisa back—alive. By your pink‑haired queen."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Combat Analysis: A - Observes Bat's style and identifies it as from Another Eden.
Deductive Reasoning: A - Connects orders to Alicia.
Strategic Insight: A - Recognizes the true mission objective.
Pattern Recognition: B - Notes the difference between this Bat and the one they knew.

Feat 11: Self‑Introduction – "The Napping Detective”

After a kick to Bat's head, Siesta introduces herself: "The Napping Detective—Siesta. I solve mysteries whenever I dream." This is the first time she has used this line in seven years, surprising Kimizuka. It demonstrates her awareness of her own image and her willingness to play the part of a detective.

Premises:

1. Kimizuka's Surprise Premise: He has never heard her say this before in seven years.
2. Siesta's Self‑Awareness Premise: She knows she is a detective and that detectives have catchphrases.
3. Theatricality Premise: She uses the line to unsettle Bat and assert her identity.
4. Role‑Playing Premise: She is playing the role of the detective, even though she is no longer officially the Ace Detective.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Defeat Bat in combat – land a clean kick to his head.
* Recognize an opportunity to establish her identity and unnerve the enemy.
* Deliver a theatrical self‑introduction – one she has apparently practiced.
* When Kimizuka reacts with surprise, she pretends it didn't happen.

Conclusion: Siesta uses theatricality as a tool for psychological impact and identity assertion.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Siesta (flashing a confident smile): "The Napping Detective—Siesta. I solve mysteries whenever I dream."

(Kimizuka: "…Siesta, what was that just now?")

Siesta (ignoring him): "Assistant. I think I'm gonna need a weapon."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Theatrical Intelligence: A - Uses a dramatic self‑introduction to establish identity.
Self‑Awareness: A - Knows her role as a detective and plays it.
Psychological Warfare: B - Uses theatrics to unnerve the enemy.
Role‑Playing: B - Adopts a detective persona.

EVENT 5: The Nighttime Walk – A Sleepless Night's Picnic (Chapter 1: A Sleepless Night's Picnic)

Feat 12: Emotional Intelligence – Reading Kimizuka's Turmoil and Creating Space for Reflection

Siesta senses that Kimizuka is troubled (from the call with Mia and Lil). She doesn't press him to talk but instead takes him for a walk, creating a space where he can process his thoughts. This is a sophisticated emotional intervention.

2. Siesta's Observation Premise: She notices his troubled expression after the call.
3. Space Premise: Sometimes people need space to think, not interrogation.

* Walk in comfortable silence, allowing him to think at his own pace.

PS: I dunno if I should've included the Ghost Train incident but I'mma skip it since I'm lazy

You know what, let me just drop a summary

The Ghost Train — Parasite Attack

Narrative Context: After leaving the church where child versions of Siesta and Nagisa are staying, Kimizuka, along with the two young girls, boards a black steam locomotive bound for the Queen's Castle (Alicia's fortress). The train resembles an old-fashioned locomotive from a bygone era. They find a private four-seater booth and settle in for what they expect to be a routine journey.

The time is approximately 1:30 PM when they board. Kimizuka buys a canned coffee, and the three of them relax in their compartment. The two girls are in good spirits—Nagisa is cheerful and excited about snacks, while Siesta seems calm and observant.

Kimizuka notices something unusual about the station staff and shopkeepers—all of them are wearing gas masks. Siesta explains that there was a mutated virus outbreak in the region a few years ago, and while it's been cleaned up, public workers still wear masks as a precaution. This establishes that Another Eden is a world plagued by constant crises, where even everyday life carries the scars of past disasters.

As the train gently sways, Kimizuka falls asleep. When he wakes up, he finds both Siesta and Nagisa leaning against his shoulders—a peaceful scene that feels oddly domestic. However, something is immediately wrong.

He checks the time on his smartphone: 2:00 AM.

He had boarded the train at 1:30 PM. He's been asleep for twelve hours. Even more troubling, the train hasn't arrived at any station, and the journey should have taken much less time.

Kimizuka shakes Siesta and Nagisa awake. Siesta immediately notices something is wrong—black smoke is gathering near the floor of the compartment. There's no fire, no burning smell, and no visible source. The smoke doesn't behave like ordinary smoke; it's more like a supernatural fog creeping into the car.

The smoke begins to affect them physically. Nagisa starts coughing violently, and Siesta pats her back in panic. The smoke is thick and suffocating, making it difficult to breathe. Kimizuka realizes this isn't a physical phenomenon—it's a mass hallucination.

Kimizuka tells everyone to close their eyes, hoping that cutting off visual input will stop the illusion. But Siesta realizes the problem: "Our brains have already accepted this phenomenon as reality." The hallucination has become real to their minds, so closing their eyes doesn't help. They can still feel the suffocation.

Despite their efforts to fight it, the smoke overwhelms them. Nagisa collapses first, then Siesta. Kimizuka struggles to stay conscious, but eventually, he also falls into unconsciousness. They're trapped in a supernatural phenomenon with no visible way out.

Just as all seems lost, a voice cuts through the darkness:

"Still clinging to this nonsense? Your fight is already over. Stop dragging innocent people into it."

A streak of pale blue light flashes through the car—a brilliant, dazzling beam that sweeps through the train. The black smoke is instantly cleared. Kimizuka opens his eyes to see someone he recognizes:

Lil (Reloaded) — the Magical Girl, standing at the other end of the car.

However, this is the Lil of Another Eden, and she doesn't know Kimizuka. She warily points her staff at him, asking who he is. Kimizuka, still groggy, lies and says she looks exactly like a character from a magical girl anime he's obsessed with. Lil calls him a "creepy otaku."

Lil explains what just happened: the black smoke was Parasite—a supernatural phenomenon similar to a vengeful spirit bound to a place. She elaborates that a virus outbreak once ravaged this region, killing many people. The collective resentment and despair of the dead took on a powerful, sentient form, creating the black smoke.

She also pastes paper talismans under their seats, presumably to prevent a recurrence.

Kimizuka pieces together the connection: the epidemic that struck this land, and the black smoke that tormented them—both are linked. The Parasite was born from a World Crisis, a massive disaster that twisted collective human will into a supernatural threat.

Just as Kimizuka is thanking Lil, she turns her attention to the sleeping Siesta and Nagisa. She points her staff at them and says:

"There's been something off this whole time. Those two—something isn't right. My instinct as a magical girl is telling me so."

Kimizuka shields them with his body, refusing to let Lil harm them. When Lil asks who he is, he replies:

"Me? I'm just your neighborhood ally of justice."

Lil is shocked by his words:

"You're really saying that… in this world, in this hell? There's no such thing as justice in this world. No hope, nothing."

Kimizuka asks her why she's still fighting, and Lil answers softly:

"For a promise I made to a friend. That was all."

Lil leaves (flying off through the window), and the train continues to its final stop. Siesta and Nagisa wake up shortly before the station arrives. Kimizuka tells them a "passing magical girl" came to their rescue, but they don't fully understand what happened.

The train arrives at a small, desolate station by the sea. From here, they must travel by boat, but because of the delay, they have to wait until the following morning. The three of them prepare to camp on the beach, starting a fire and warming up soup.

Significance of the Incident

1. Revelation of Another Eden's Nature: The ghost train demonstrates how this parallel world is plagued by crises born from human despair. The virus outbreak and the resulting Parasite show how the world is slowly being destroyed by accumulated suffering.
2. Lil's Role in Another Eden: This world's Lil is still a magical girl, but she's been worn down by the hopelessness. She fights not for justice, but for a promise to a friend (implied to be Mia). This contrasts sharply with the Lil of the original world.
3. Child Siesta's Unconscious Awareness: After the incident, Siesta expresses frustration and a sense of inadequacy, saying she feels like she "used to have some kind of special role" and that she "should have been the one to deal with that situation." This hints at her true nature as a program carrying the essence of the Ace Detective.
4. Kimizuka's Protective Role: He shields the girls from Lil's suspicion, reaffirming his role as someone who protects detectives—even in a world where they're just children.
5. Hope vs. Despair: The incident serves as a microcosm of Another Eden's conflict: despair (the Parasite) versus hope (Lil's intervention, Kimizuka's resolve). The title of the next section—"Can the world be saved with an aurora?"—asks whether beauty and hope can endure in a dying world.

Later that night, as they camp by the sea, an aurora appears in the dark sky. It's actually a side effect of SYSTEM's maintenance, but it's beautiful nonetheless. Siesta reflects that sports, music, paintings, and novels are disappearing from this world—things that "aren't necessary to survive" are gradually vanishing. Yet, she says:

"Even in a world like this, we still take each other's hands and keep moving forward."

She takes Nagisa's hand, and for a brief moment, there's hope.

Feat 23: Quick Thinking – "We Just Need to Stand Out" (Finding the Lost Boy)

On the Ashen Continent, when they need to find a lost boy in volcanic ash, Siesta quickly proposes: "We just need to stand out." She suggests shoulder rides to make them more visible. This is a simple but effective practical solution.

Premises:

1. Problem Premise: Visibility is limited by volcanic ash.
2. Solution Premise: Making themselves taller will increase visibility.
3. Practicality Premise: Shoulder rides are a quick, no‑cost solution.
4. Teamwork Premise: They can take turns.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Assess the problem – low visibility due to ash.
* Recognize that height increases visibility.
* Propose shoulder rides – a simple, immediate solution.
* Implement the solution – they take turns.

Conclusion: Child Siesta is practical and quick‑thinking.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Kimizuka: "But… how are we supposed to find him? The ash severely limited visibility."

Siesta: "We just need to stand out."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Practical Reasoning: A (Quickly identifies a simple solution.)
Problem‑Solving: A (Proposes shoulder rides to increase visibility.)
Teamwork: B (Suggests taking turns.)

Feat 24: Logical Argument – Clashing with Nagisa About Priorities

When Nagisa wants to help villagers instead of heading to the castle, Siesta argues: "We need to figure out who we really are first. Whether it's helping people or whatever other mission we might have… all of that can come after we get our memories back." This demonstrates logical prioritization.

Premises:

1. Identity Premise: They don't know who they are.
2. Prioritization Premise: Identity should come before action.
3. Goal Premise: Their main goal is to reach the castle.
4. Emotion vs. Logic Premise: Nagisa is acting on emotion; Siesta is acting on logic.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Recognize that they lack memories.
* Argue that they need to know who they are before helping others.
* Prioritize the castle mission – it is their primary objective.
* Clash with Nagisa, who prioritizes immediate help.

Conclusion: Child Siesta prioritizes identity and mission over immediate emotional gratification.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Siesta: "We need to figure out who we really are first. Whether it's helping people or whatever other mission we might have… all of that can come after we get our memories back."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Logical Reasoning: A (Prioritizes identity before action.)
Goal Orientation: A (Keeps the main mission in focus.)
Emotional vs. Logical: A (Argues for logic over emotion.)

Feat 25: Sacrifice – Taking the Sword for Kimizuka

In the Nightless Castle, when Olivia tries to kill Kimizuka, child Siesta steps in and takes the sword to her chest. She sacrifices herself to protect him, demonstrating her inherent heroism and selflessness.

Premises:

1. Threat Premise: Olivia is about to kill Kimizuka.
2. Protective Instinct Premise: Siesta wants to protect him.
3. Sacrifice Premise: She is willing to die for him.
4. Heroism Premise: This is who she is – a hero, even as a program.

Chain of Reasoning:

* See Olivia attack Kimizuka.
* Recognize he cannot defend himself – he is frozen.
* Step in front of the sword – physical protection.
* Accept the wound – she does not flinch.
* Reassure him as she fades.

Conclusion: Child Siesta is selflessly heroic.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

(Siesta steps in front of the sword, pierced through her chest.)

Siesta (to Kimizuka): "See? It's not like I'm some see‑through ghost, right?"

(Later, as she fades) "Don't give up hope. In this broken world, once you lose that, you'll never be able to move forward again."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Heroism: S (Sacrifices herself for another.)
Emotional Strength: A (Maintains hope even as she fades.)
Courage: S (Steps in front of a sword.)

Feat 26: Meta‑Awareness – Recognizing Kimizuka as a Time Traveler

When Kimizuka accidentally travels back in time to the SPES facility, child Siesta is the only one who can see and hear him. She explains: "I've got this little special ability, you see? When someone wanders into a place they're not supposed to be… I can talk to them like this." This is the power of "daydream."

Premises:

1. Time Travel Premise: Kimizuka is in the past.
2. Special Ability Premise: Siesta can perceive him.
3. Daydream Premise: This is the power she has.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Perceive Kimizuka's presence – he is not supposed to be there.
* Recognize he is from another time.
* Explain her special ability – she can perceive anomalies.
* Communicate with him despite the time gap.

Conclusion: Child Siesta has the power of daydream – the ability to perceive what others cannot.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Siesta: "I've got this little special ability, you see? When someone wanders into a place they're not supposed to be… I can talk to them like this."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Special Ability Knowledge: A (Understands her own power.)

Feat 27: Emotional Catharsis – "Are You Stupid, Kimi?" (The Final Goodbye)

As child Siesta fades away, she tells Kimizuka: "Are you stupid, Kimi? … Maybe a simple wish can't rewrite fate. I know that. But even so, that's no reason to stop hoping. … And even if we fail, our legacy… will surely live on in someone else." This is the same catchphrase she uses in the real world, showing that even as a program, she carries the detective's essence.

Premises:

1. Fate Premise: Simple wishes can't rewrite fate.
2. Hope Premise: Even so, hope is important.
3. Legacy Premise: Even if they fail, their will will live on.
4. Catchphrase Premise: "Are you stupid, Kimi?" is her signature line.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Acknowledge that fate cannot be changed.
* Argue that hope is still important.
* Assert that their legacy will continue.
* Use her signature line to maintain character.

Conclusion: Child Siesta embodies the detective's will even as a program.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Kimizuka: "I'm sorry I couldn't save you. … I wanted to save you… I wanted to save Nagisa! I just wanted you both to live!"

Siesta: "Are you stupid, Kimi? Maybe a simple wish can't rewrite fate. I know that. But even so, that's no reason to stop hoping. It's not a reason for me to give up either. I'm sure the me of tomorrow, and every day after, will keep struggling, keep fighting to the very end. … And even if we fail, our legacy… will surely live on in someone else."

(She ties a red ribbon around his finger and vanishes.)

Siesta (final words): "I'll be waiting, Kimihiko."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Wisdom: S (Understands fate but still hopes.)
Legacy Awareness: S (Knows that will lives on.)

EVENT 9: The Epilogue – The Cherry Blossom Scene and Final Line

Context: After the Great Cataclysm is resolved and the world returns to normal, the group holds a cherry blossom viewing party. Siesta is present, along with Kimizuka, Nagisa, and others. Kimizuka reflects on his journey.

Feat 28: Understanding That Kimizuka Still Feels Unfulfilled – Offering to Wait

When Kimizuka mentions he had something important to tell Siesta "when this case is over," and then hesitates, Siesta immediately accepts this without pressure, saying "It's okay. I'll wait for you, no matter how long it takes."

Premises:

1. Kimizuka's Hesitation Premise: He is not ready to say what he wants to say.
2. Nagisa's Consideration Premise: Nagisa leaves to give them privacy.
3. Siesta's Awareness Premise: She knows what he wants to say.
4. Patience Premise: She is willing to wait.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Observe Kimizuka's awkwardness.
* Recognize that pushing him would be counterproductive.
* Reassure him verbally: "It's okay. I'll wait for you, no matter how long it takes."
* Add "You two are the ones who gave me tomorrow."

Conclusion: Siesta demonstrates patience and emotional maturity.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Kimizuka: "My bad, Siesta. It seems that it's not the time yet."

Siesta: "It's okay. I'll wait for you, no matter how long it takes. You two are the ones who gave me tomorrow."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Emotional Intelligence: S (Reads Kimizuka's hesitation and respects it.)
Patience: A (Willing to wait indefinitely.)
Gratitude Expression: B (Acknowledges that Nagisa and Kimizuka saved her future.)
Relationship Wisdom: S (Knows that forcing a confession would ruin it.)

Feat 29: Final Line – "It's Just Love at First Sight, Stupid Kimi”

In the final lines, Kimizuka reflects on their history. Siesta says: "Singularity? What's that? It's just love at first sight, stupid Kimi." This reframes the entire series – the grand cosmic machinery is secondary to a simple human emotion.

Premises:

1. Kimizuka's Logical Explanation Premise: He believes she chose him because of his Singularity nature.
2. The Rationalization Premise: That explanation was a convenient cover.
3. The True Feeling Premise: The real reason is simpler: she fell in love with him.
4. Meta‑Narrative Premise: The entire series is built on this foundation.
5. Humor as Delivery Premise: She says it in a teasing tone.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Hear Kimizuka's thoughts about the Singularity.
* Decide to finally tell him the truth.
* Dismiss the "Singularity" concept with feigned ignorance.
* State the simple truth: "It's just love at first sight."
* Add "stupid Kimi" – a term of endearment disguised as an insult.
* Walk away without waiting for a response.

Conclusion: This line redefines the entire series – human emotion is the true engine of the story.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Kimizuka (thinking): "Siesta. You made a choice that day, remember? You chose the Singularity. … I wanted us to depend on each other a little bit more."

Siesta (as if responding): "Singularity? What's that? It's just love at first sight, stupid Kimi." (She chuckles and walks away.)

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Emotional Honesty: S (Abandons rational explanations for a vulnerable confession.)
Humor: A (Delivers the line with a teasing tone.)
Relationship Wisdom: S (Knows the real reason is emotional, not logical.)
Meta‑Narrative Awareness: S (Understands the story's foundation is a personal choice.)
Timing: A (Chooses the perfect moment for revelation.)

Overall Cognitive Tier for Volume 13: S (Genius) – Siesta demonstrates a wide range of cognitive abilities across multiple temporal contexts: practical survival in the present, strategic deduction in the Great Cataclysm flashback, and emotional intelligence and heroism in the Another Eden flashback. Her deduction that Abel hid the Akashic Records inside Yggdrasil is the volume's most impressive logical feat, solving the central mystery of the Great Cataclysm. The final line – "It's just love at first sight" – is not just a romantic confession but a meta‑cognitive dismissal of the entire logical framework that has governed the series, revealing that human emotion is the true engine of the story. The child Siesta sequences show that even as a program, her core traits – protectiveness, quick thinking, heroism, and the detective's will – are inseparable from her identity.

Code: RED

Note on Code: RED: This is a side story focused on Charlotte Arisaka Anderson and her partnership with the mage "Charnelia" (actually Charlotte's twin sister). Siesta appears in flashbacks, Charlotte's memories, and a daydream sequence. Though Siesta is dead for most of the volume, her cognitive feats are demonstrated through her pre‑death actions: resolving the hijacking, training Charlotte, writing the letter, and identifying the Detective Killer. The volume also reveals significant new information about Siesta's psychological insight and her strategic planning for her own death. Her legacy drives the entire plot, making her a constant presence even in absence.

EVENT 1: The Hijacking Incident – Siesta's Pre‑emptive Resolution (Prologue)

Context: Three and a half years before the main story, Siesta infiltrates a hijacked plane, defeats the pseudohuman hijacker, and lands the plane by performing an "impossible" drift with the aircraft. Charlotte watches from afar, having been planted on the plane to monitor the situation. This is the first time Charlotte witnesses Siesta's true capabilities and establishes the foundation of their master‑disciple relationship. The incident is a microcosm of Siesta's entire philosophy: resolving incidents before they occur, using intelligence and preparation rather than brute force.

Feat 1: Pre‑emptive Incident Resolution – Identifying and Neutralizing a Threat Before It Fully Manifests

Siesta is described as having "resolved the case before it even occurred." She knew the plane would be hijacked before takeoff and planted Charlotte on board. She then neutralized the hijacker and executed an emergency landing. Her catchphrase: "First‑rate detectives resolve incidents before they even occur, you see." This is the core of her identity as the Ace Detective and demonstrates a level of foresight that borders on prescience.

Premises:

1. Foreknowledge Premise: She knew the hijacking would happen before the plane took off. This indicates access to intelligence (likely through the organization or her own information network) or predictive reasoning based on pattern recognition.
2. Intelligence Source Premise: She likely had sources within the supernatural underworld or the organization that provided advance warning.
3. Threat Assessment Premise: She assessed that the hijacker was a pseudohuman – a supernatural being – and that normal security measures would fail.
4. Resource Deployment Premise: She planted Charlotte on the plane as an asset – a 14‑year‑old agent who could monitor the situation and provide backup if needed.
5. Infiltration Premise: She herself was on the plane to act, ready to engage the hijacker directly.
6. Neutralization Premise: She defeated the pseudohuman hijacker – a supernatural enemy beyond normal human capabilities.
7. Emergency Landing Premise: She landed the plane despite critical damage (vertical stabilizer destroyed by magecraft).
8. Zero Casualties Premise: All 600 passengers and crew survived.
9. Information Control Premise: She arranged for witness memory erasure to maintain the secrecy of the supernatural.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Step 1: Receive intelligence about the hijacking plot – likely through the organization or her own network.
* Step 2: Assess that normal security measures would fail against a pseudohuman – they are beyond conventional countermeasures.
* Step 3: Recognize that a pre‑emptive strike is the only way to guarantee zero casualties.
* Step 4: Board the plane herself – the only way to guarantee success.
* Step 5: Plant Charlotte as a backup – a trusted agent who can observe and act if needed.
* Step 6: Wait for the hijacker to act – observe and analyze their behavior, confirming their nature and intentions.
* Step 7: Neutralize the threat – defeat the pseudohuman without endangering passengers.
* Step 8: Execute an emergency landing despite damage – using skills from a flight simulator, demonstrating adaptability.
* Step 9: Ensure zero casualties – the primary objective.
* Step 10: Arrange for witness memory erasure – the organization's standard procedure to protect the secret.

Conclusion: Siesta resolves the incident before it can claim lives, fulfilling her role as a first‑rate detective. She demonstrates pre‑emptive planning, combat proficiency, crisis management, and information control. This establishes her as someone who operates on a level far beyond ordinary detectives – she doesn't just solve crimes, she prevents them.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

(Announcement over PA): "Is there a detective on the plane?"

Charlotte: "There is. The world's greatest ace detective."

(Later, on the runway)

Siesta: "I'm back, Charl. A bit nerve‑wracking, isn't it?"

Charlotte: "I was really worried this time, Ma'am."

Siesta: "Don't be sulky, Charl. … The case has been solved. Zero casualties… Let's make sure the witnesses forget everything cleanly."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Pre‑emptive Planning: S (Resolves incident before it happens – the hallmark of an Ace Detective.)
Operational Coordination: A (Plants Charlotte as an asset.)
Crisis Management: S (Lands a damaged plane with zero casualties.)
Information Control: A (Arranges for witness memory erasure.)
Combat Proficiency: S (Defeats a pseudohuman.)
Threat Assessment: S (Identifies the supernatural nature of the threat in advance.)
Intelligence Gathering: A (Sources advance warning.)

Feat 2: Combat Analysis – Defeating a Pseudohuman with Minimal Effort

Siesta defeated the pseudohuman hijacker. The details are not shown, but Charlotte's reaction indicates it was done with surgical precision. The hijacker was described as having "no intention of dying," suggesting suicidal intent, yet Siesta neutralized them without casualties. This implies Siesta understood the hijacker's psychology and exploited it.

Premises:

1. Hijacker's Nature Premise: The hijacker was a pseudohuman (supernatural being) – beyond normal human capabilities.
2. Suicidal Intent Premise: The hijacker had "no intention of dying" – meaning they were willing to die in the attack, possibly as a martyr or to cover up the organization behind them.
3. Siesta's Skill Premise: She is an Ace Detective with years of experience fighting "Enemies of the world."
4. Psychological Insight Premise: Siesta understood the hijacker's mindset – they were fanatical, not rational.
5. Restraint Premise: She neutralized them without causing collateral damage – no passengers harmed.
6. Charlotte's Reaction Premise: Charlotte, a trained agent, was impressed and relieved.

Chain of Reasoning (inferred from Charlotte's observations):

* Step 1: Identify the hijacker's supernatural nature – observe their abilities.
* Step 2: Recognize the threat level – pseudohumans require special handling.
* Step 3: Understand the hijacker's psychology – suicidal intent, fanaticism.
* Step 4: Engage the hijacker with appropriate force – not excessive, not insufficient.
* Step 5: Exploit their psychology – possibly by creating a situation where they are forced to reveal themselves.
* Step 6: Defeat them without causing collateral damage – passengers and crew must be protected.
* Step 7: Ensure the hijacker is neutralized – they cannot continue their attack.

Conclusion: Siesta's combat skills are elite, demonstrated by her ability to neutralize a supernatural threat in a confined space (a passenger plane) with zero casualties. Her psychological insight into the enemy's mindset allowed her to defeat them efficiently.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

(Charlotte, upon seeing Siesta emerge from the wreckage): "In front of her stood the true 'Ace Detective' who fought against 'Enemies of the world'."

(Later): Siesta: "We don't have a mission (Code:RED) today. It's our day off."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Combat Proficiency: S (Defeats a pseudohuman.)
Threat Assessment: A (Recognizes the supernatural nature of the enemy.)
Restraint: A (Neutralizes without excessive force.)
Psychological Insight: A (Understands the enemy's mindset.)
Tactical Execution: S (Executes flawlessly in a confined space.)

Feat 3: Crisis Management – Landing a Damaged Plane

Siesta landed a passenger plane that had lost its vertical stabilizer (destroyed by magecraft). She performed a 180‑degree drift at over 200 km/h, scraping the plane along the runway to stop it. She learned piloting from a flight simulator at a Japanese game center – demonstrating rapid skill acquisition and improvisation. This is a remarkable feat of spatial reasoning, physics understanding, and risk assessment.

Premises:

1. Damage Premise: The plane's vertical stabilizer was destroyed – critical for directional control. The plane was effectively uncontrollable.
2. Landing Premise: A normal landing was impossible – the plane would overshoot the runway.
3. Skill Acquisition Premise: She learned piloting from a flight simulator a year ago – not formal training.
4. Improvisation Premise: She performed a drift – a non‑standard maneuver not taught in standard pilot training.
5. Time Pressure Premise: The plane was running out of runway – she had seconds to act.
6. Safety Premise: She had 600 lives in her hands.
7. Physics Understanding Premise: She understood the forces involved – inertia, friction, drag.
8. Spatial Awareness Premise: She visualized the drift and its execution in real time. Also knew the exact location and had Charlie positioned there beforehand hence showcasing her insane foresight.

Chain of Reasoning (inferred from the scene):

* Step 1: Assess the damage – the vertical stabilizer is gone, causing loss of directional control.
* Step 2: Determine that a normal landing is impossible – the plane will overshoot the runway.
* Step 3: Calculate the remaining runway length and speed – she has seconds.
* Step 4: Recall flight simulator knowledge – drifting is possible at high speed with proper control inputs.
* Step 5: Execute the drift – turn the plane 180 degrees to use the runway length backward.
* Step 6: Allow the plane to scrape along the runway to reduce speed – sacrificing the landing gear and body.
* Step 7: Stop the plane safely – preventing collision with the control tower.
* Step 8: Ensure all passengers and crew survive.

Conclusion: Siesta demonstrates exceptional crisis management and improvisation, using skills learned from a game to save 600 lives. This is a testament to her ability to apply knowledge from any source to real‑world problems, and her coolness under pressure.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Charlotte: "By the way, Ma'am, where did you learn how to pilot?"

Siesta: "Flight simulator. At a Japanese game center a year ago."

Charlotte: "Eh?"

Siesta: "...Eh...?"

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Crisis Management: S (Lands a damaged plane with zero casualties.)
Rapid Skill Acquisition: A (Learned piloting from a simulator.)
Improvisation: S (Performs an unconventional drift maneuver.)
Spatial Awareness: A (Visualizes the drift and its execution.)
Risk Assessment: A (Calculates the margin for error.)
Physics Understanding: A (Understands forces and friction.)

Feat 4: Information Control – Arranging Witness Memory Erasure

After the landing, Siesta states: "Let's make sure the witnesses forget everything cleanly." This indicates she has arranged for memory erasure – either through the organization or her own resources. She understands the importance of maintaining secrecy about the supernatural and the need to protect the passengers from psychological trauma.

Premises:

1. Secrecy Premise: The existence of the supernatural must be hidden from the public.
2. Witness Premise: There were 600 passengers and crew who witnessed impossible events.
3. Memory Erasure Premise: The organization has methods to erase or alter memories.
4. Siesta's Authority Premise: She has the authority to arrange this.
5. Psychological Protection Premise: Witnesses may be traumatized by the impossible; erasure protects them.
6. Operational Security Premise: Loose memories could lead to leaks.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Step 1: Recognize that the passengers witnessed impossible phenomena – the drift, the magecraft attack.
* Step 2: Understand the need to maintain secrecy – the organization's primary purpose.
* Step 3: Recognize that witnesses may be traumatized – erasure protects their mental health.
* Step 4: Arrange for memory erasure – the standard procedure.
* Step 5: Ensure no one remembers what they saw – protecting the order of the world.

Conclusion: Siesta controls the information environment as part of her detective work, ensuring the supernatural remains hidden and witnesses are protected.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Siesta: "The case has been solved. Zero casualties… Let's make sure the witnesses forget everything cleanly."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Information Control: A (Arranges for witness memory erasure.)
Operational Awareness: A (Understands the need for secrecy.)
Authority: A (Has the power to arrange memory erasure.)
Psychological Protection: A (Protects witnesses from trauma.)
Ethical Reasoning: A (Recognizes the need for both secrecy and protection.)

Feat 5: Relationship Building – Establishing Trust with Charlotte

After the incident, Siesta comforts Charlotte, who was worried. She calls her "sulky," teases her, and strokes her hair, saying "good girl." This is not just affection – it's strategic relationship building. Siesta is grooming Charlotte as her disciple, recognizing her potential and nurturing loyalty.

Premises:

1. Charlotte's Worry Premise: Charlotte was genuinely worried about Siesta's safety – she was attached.
2. Siesta's Awareness Premise: Siesta knows Charlotte cares deeply about her.
3. Disciple Relationship Premise: Siesta is training Charlotte to inherit her legacy.
4. Affection as Bonding Premise: Physical affection builds trust and loyalty.
5. Teasing as Connection Premise: Teasing lightens the mood and establishes a playful dynamic.
6. Reinforcement Premise: "Good girl" reinforces Charlotte's behavior and builds her confidence.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Step 1: Observe Charlotte's worry – she is emotionally invested.
* Step 2: Acknowledge her concern – but don't dwell on it, to avoid making it a burden.
* Step 3: Use teasing to lighten the mood – "Don't be sulky, Charl."
* Step 4: Use physical affection – stroking her hair, which is calming.
* Step 5: Say "good girl" – reinforcing positive behavior and building Charlotte's self‑worth.
* Step 6: This builds a strong emotional bond that will last beyond Siesta's death.

Conclusion: Siesta uses affection and teasing to build a strong master‑disciple relationship with Charlotte. This bond is the foundation of Charlotte's later resolve to inherit Siesta's legacy.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Charlotte: "I was really worried this time, Ma'am."

Siesta: "Don't be sulky, Charl."

Charlotte: "I'm not sulking...!"

Siesta: "Then, are you mad?" (She placed her hand on Charlotte's head and stroked her blonde hair, saying "good girl.")

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Relationship Building: A (Uses affection to build trust.)
Emotional Intelligence: A (Recognizes Charlotte's emotional state.)
Mentorship: A (Grooms Charlotte as her disciple.)
Reinforcement: A (Uses positive reinforcement to build confidence.)

EVENT 2: Siesta's Letter to Charlotte – Emotional Intelligence in Her Final Message (Chapter 0: The Last Letter — Mislead)

Context: After Siesta's death, Charlotte receives a letter. Siesta's final message demonstrates deep understanding of Charlotte's emotional state and provides guidance for her future. The letter also contains a critical mislead – Siesta asks Charlotte to defeat the "Detective Killer," which sets Charlotte on her path. The letter is titled "The Last Letter (Mislead)," indicating it is deliberately incomplete.

Feat 6: Psychological Insight – Anticipating Charlotte's Grief and Guilt

Siesta's letter anticipates Charlotte's reactions: she will be angry, sad, and will blame herself. Siesta explicitly addresses these feelings, reassuring Charlotte that her death is not Charlotte's fault. She then gives Charlotte a mission – defeat the "Detective Killer" – to channel her grief into purpose. This is a masterful understanding of Charlotte's psychology.

Premises:

1. Charlotte's Emotional State Premise: Charlotte will be devastated by Siesta's death – she was deeply attached to her "Ma'am."
2. Charlotte's Guilt Premise: Charlotte will blame herself (the "curse" – a self‑perceived jinx that everyone she works with dies).
3. Siesta's Awareness Premise: Siesta knows Charlotte's psychology intimately – they worked together for years.
4. Letter as Communication Premise: A letter can convey final thoughts and guidance.
5. Grief Management Premise: People need purpose to overcome grief – without it, they may fall into despair.
6. Future Orientation Premise: Giving a mission helps Charlotte look forward rather than dwell on the past.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Step 1: Know that Charlotte will grieve deeply – she is emotionally dependent on Siesta.
* Step 2: Know that Charlotte will blame herself – she has a tendency toward self‑blame (the "curse").
* Step 3: Address both emotions directly in the letter – don't leave them unspoken.
* Step 4: Assure Charlotte that her death is not Charlotte's fault – "my decision to die has nothing to do with your curse."
* Step 5: Give Charlotte a mission – defeat the Detective Killer.
* Step 6: This channels grief into purpose, preventing Charlotte from being consumed by despair.
* Step 7: Trust that Charlotte will rise to the challenge.

Conclusion: Siesta uses her final message to guide Charlotte through grief and give her purpose. This demonstrates profound psychological insight and strategic thinking about Charlotte's future.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

"Dear Charlotte, … I can guess how you're feeling right now… you're mad, right. You must be really angry. I'm sorry. I'm sorry I died. But please remember this one thing. First, my decision to die has nothing to do with your curse."

"So, this is my last selfish request. Please, wipe away your tears, and inherit my will. I want you to defeat the 'Detective Killer' who vanquished me."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Psychological Insight: S (Anticipates Charlotte's emotional responses with precision.)
Emotional Communication: S (Addresses grief, guilt, and purpose in a single message.)
Strategic Guidance: A (Channels grief into a mission.)
Empathy: S (Understands Charlotte's need for purpose after loss.)
Long‑term Planning: A (Prepares Charlotte for life after Siesta.)

Feat 7: Strategic Misdirection – Setting Charlotte on a Path

The letter asks Charlotte to defeat the "Detective Killer." This is a mislead – the "Detective Killer" is not a single person but a title, and the true culprit is Togenkyo. However, the letter's purpose is to give Charlotte a goal, not to provide accurate information. The title "Mislead" indicates the letter is deliberately incomplete. Siesta trusted that Charlotte would uncover the truth on her own.

Premises:

1. Charlotte's Need Premise: Charlotte needs a purpose to survive grief.
2. Detective Killer Premise: There is a "Detective Killer" – a threat to detectives.
3. Misdirection Premise: Siesta may not have known all the details but trusted Charlotte to uncover the truth.
4. Incomplete Information Premise: The letter is not the complete answer – it's a starting point.
5. Growth Premise: Charlotte will grow by investigating and discovering the truth herself.
6. Trust Premise: Siesta trusts Charlotte's abilities to figure it out.

Chain of Reasoning (inferred):

* Step 1: Recognize that Charlotte needs a mission to channel her grief.
* Step 2: Give her a goal – defeat the Detective Killer.
* Step 3: Provide only partial information – enough to set her on the right track.
* Step 4: Trust that Charlotte will uncover the truth through her own investigation.
* Step 5: The letter is a catalyst, not a complete answer.
* Step 6: This sets Charlotte on a path of growth and discovery.

Conclusion: Siesta's letter is strategically designed to give Charlotte purpose and direction, even if the specific information is incomplete. This demonstrates trust in Charlotte's abilities and a belief in her potential.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

"So, this is my last selfish request. Please, wipe away your tears, and inherit my will. I want you to defeat the 'Detective Killer' who vanquished me."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Strategic Misdirection: A (Gives Charlotte a goal without complete information.)
Long‑term Planning: A (Sets Charlotte on a path of investigation.)
Trust in Others: A (Trusts Charlotte to uncover the truth.)
Information Management: A (Provides incomplete but directionally correct information.)
Growth Facilitation: A (Allows Charlotte to develop her own investigative skills.)

Feat 8: Final Gift – The Pendant as a Token of Affection

Siesta gave Charlotte a pendant with a blue jewel that matches her own eyes. This is not just a gift – it's a symbol of their connection and a tangible reminder of Siesta's legacy. Charlotte wears it throughout Code: RED, and it becomes a source of strength.

Premises:

1. Symbolism Premise: The pendant represents Siesta's connection to Charlotte.
2. Gift Premise: It was given before Siesta's death.
3. Charlotte's Attachment Premise: Charlotte treasures it.
4. Emotional Anchor Premise: The pendant serves as an emotional anchor in times of distress.

Chain of Reasoning (inferred):

* Step 1: Choose a gift that symbolizes their relationship – a blue jewel matching Siesta's eyes.
* Step 2: Give it to Charlotte as a token of affection.
* Step 3: The pendant becomes a reminder of Siesta's legacy.
* Step 4: It serves as a source of strength for Charlotte when she feels lost.

Conclusion: The pendant is a physical symbol of Siesta's legacy and Charlotte's mission, providing emotional support.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

(Charlotte's thoughts): "Holding in her hand the keepsake pendant filled with precious memories."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Symbolic Communication: A (Uses the pendant to represent their connection.)
Emotional Intelligence: A (Understands the importance of tangible reminders.)
Gift Selection: A (Chooses a meaningful and personal item.)

EVENT 3: Charlotte's Daydream – The Final Conversation with Siesta

Context: During her confrontation with the mage Charlotte (actually Charnelia's sister), Charlotte Arisaka Anderson experiences a daydream where she meets Siesta in a shopping mall. This is a psychological confrontation – Siesta challenges Charlotte's determination to inherit her legacy. The daydream is a manifestation of Charlotte's subconscious, but it represents Siesta's lasting influence and Charlotte's internal conflict.

Feat 9: Psychological Confrontation – Forcing Charlotte to Face Her Own Weakness

In the daydream, Siesta presents a challenge: Charlotte claims she wants to inherit Siesta's legacy, but Siesta points out that Charlotte has decided never to make friends – a decision born of fear, not strength. This confrontation forces Charlotte to examine her own motivations and recognize her fear of loss.

Premises:

1. Charlotte's Fear Premise: Charlotte is afraid of losing people, so she avoids making friends.
2. Legacy Premise: Inheriting Siesta's legacy requires facing fear and building connections.
3. Contradiction Premise: Avoiding friendship contradicts the ideals of justice (which requires connection).
4. Daydream Premise: This is a psychological confrontation – a manifestation of Charlotte's internal conflict.
5. Siesta as Proxy Premise: The daydream Siesta represents Charlotte's own conscience.
6. Self‑Deception Premise: Charlotte has convinced herself that isolation is strength.

Chain of Reasoning (Siesta's dialogue as a proxy for Charlotte's subconscious):

* Step 1: Listen to Charlotte's claim about inheriting her legacy.
* Step 2: Point out the contradiction: Charlotte has sworn never to make friends.
* Step 3: Identify the root cause: fear, not strength.
* Step 4: Challenge Charlotte: "Do you really think you can defeat me, Charlotte, all by yourself?"
* Step 5: Push her to confront her fear.
* Step 6: This is not about defeating Charlotte but about forcing her to grow.
* Step 7: The confrontation is a catalyst for Charlotte's eventual acceptance of Charnelia.

Conclusion: Siesta uses the daydream to force Charlotte to examine her own motivations and fears. This is not a literal conversation but a psychological catalyst that enables Charlotte's growth.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Siesta: "Charlotte, your true foe is the legacy of the Ace Detective. … You said you didn't need friends anymore. You said you didn't want to lose anyone anymore. But that means you have no intention of overcoming your own weakness. … Do you really think you can defeat me, Charlotte, all by yourself?"

Charlotte: "I'm different now."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Psychological Insight: S (Identifies Charlotte's fear as the root of her isolation.)
Socratic Questioning: S (Forces Charlotte to confront her own contradictions.)
Motivational Challenge: A (Pushes Charlotte to overcome her weakness.)
Internal Conflict Resolution: A (Catalyzes Charlotte's self‑awareness.)

Feat 10: Emotional Catalyst – Pushing Charlotte to Accept Help

Siesta's challenge is not meant to defeat Charlotte but to push her toward growth. She knows that Charlotte will eventually need to accept help from others – specifically, from Charnelia. The confrontation prepares Charlotte to accept partnership by demonstrating the limitations of isolation.

Premises:

1. Charlotte's Limitations Premise: Alone, Charlotte cannot inherit the Ace Detective's legacy – she lacks mystic knowledge.
2. Teamwork Premise: She needs a partner to succeed – someone who complements her skills.
3. Siesta's Knowledge Premise: Siesta knows about Charnelia (or trusts that Charlotte will find a partner).
4. Growth Premise: Charlotte must overcome her fear of friendship to succeed.
5. Catalyst Premise: The daydream plants the seed of acceptance.

Chain of Reasoning (inferred):

* Step 1: Challenge Charlotte's isolation – make her see its limitations.
* Step 2: Force her to realize that she cannot succeed alone.
* Step 3: This prepares her to accept Charnelia as a partner.
* Step 4: The daydream plants the seed of acceptance.
* Step 5: Charlotte's subsequent actions show she has internalized the lesson.

Conclusion: Siesta's daydream sets the stage for Charlotte's eventual acceptance of Charnelia as a partner, enabling her to grow beyond her fear.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

(At the end of the daydream)

Siesta: "Charlotte, you should do whatever you want to do."

(Charlotte wakes up, determined.)

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Emotional Catalyst: S (Uses confrontation to enable growth.)
Strategic Foresight: A (Anticipates Charlotte's need for a partner.)
Trust in Others: A (Believes Charlotte will find her own path.)
Mentorship: S (Guides Charlotte toward growth.)

Feat 11: Meta‑Awareness – "A First‑rate Detective Resolves Incidents Before They Even Occur"

In the daydream, Siesta repeats her catchphrase: "A first‑rate detective resolves incidents before they even occur, you see." She touches the barrel of her empty musket to Charlotte's pendant, demonstrating that she has already "defeated" Charlotte by predicting her actions. This is a meta‑awareness of her own role as a detective and a demonstration of her predictive ability.

Premises:

1. Catchphrase Premise: Siesta's catchphrase defines her identity.
2. Empty Musket Premise: Her musket is empty – she has no intention of shooting.
3. Predictive Ability Premise: She has already predicted Charlotte's actions.
4. Victory Premise: She wins without firing a shot.
5. Symbolism Premise: Touching the pendant symbolizes her connection to Charlotte.
6. Self‑Awareness Premise: She understands that her power lies in prediction, not force.

Chain of Reasoning (Siesta's internal logic):

* Step 1: Know that Charlotte will hesitate to shoot – she cannot bring herself to fire.
* Step 2: Use the empty musket as a symbol – she doesn't need to fire.
* Step 3: Touch Charlotte's pendant – a symbolic "defeat" without violence.
* Step 4: Repeat the catchphrase – reinforcing her identity.
* Step 5: This demonstrates that she has predicted Charlotte's actions perfectly.

Conclusion: Siesta demonstrates meta‑awareness of her own role and abilities. She wins the confrontation without firing a shot, proving her catchphrase true.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Siesta: "And once again, you will lose to me. A first-rate detective... resolves incidents before they even occur, you see." (She touches her gun's barrel to Charlotte's pendant.)

(In the end, there was no gunshot. The musket was empty.)

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Meta‑Awareness: S (Understands her own role as a detective.)
Predictive Ability: S (Predicts Charlotte's hesitation.)
Symbolic Communication: A (Uses the empty musket as a symbol.)
Psychological Dominance: S (Wins without firing a shot.)
Self‑Reflection: S (Understands her own methodology.)

Feat 12: Final Guidance – "Charlotte, You Should Do Whatever You Want to Do”

At the end of the daydream, Siesta tells Charlotte: "Charlotte, you should do whatever you want to do." This is not a dismissal – it's a final release. Siesta is telling Charlotte that she no longer needs to live in Siesta's shadow; she can forge her own path. This is the ultimate mentorship: enabling independence.

Premises:

1. Charlotte's Dependence Premise: Charlotte has been defined by her devotion to Siesta.
2. Release Premise: Siesta is releasing Charlotte from that devotion.
3. Growth Premise: Charlotte must now make her own choices.
4. Trust Premise: Siesta trusts Charlotte to make the right decisions.
5. Freedom Premise: True growth comes from independence.

Chain of Reasoning (inferred):

* Step 1: Recognize that Charlotte is trapped by her devotion.
* Step 2: Release her from that trap – she can choose her own path.
* Step 3: Trust that Charlotte will make the right choices.
* Step 4: This allows Charlotte to grow beyond Siesta's shadow.
* Step 5: It also fulfills Siesta's role as a mentor – to enable independence.

Conclusion: Siesta's final words release Charlotte from her devotion, allowing her to forge her own path and become her own person.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

Siesta: "Charlotte, you should do whatever you want to do."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Mentorship: S (Releases Charlotte from devotion.)
Emotional Intelligence: S (Recognizes Charlotte's need for independence.)
Trust in Others: A (Believes Charlotte will make the right choices.)
Growth Facilitation: S (Enables Charlotte's independence.)

EVENT 4: The Ace Detective's Legacy – Siesta's Influence on the Plot

Context: Though Siesta is dead throughout most of Code: RED, her legacy drives the plot. Charlotte's mission to inherit Siesta's will, her investigation of Siesta's legacy, and the actions of the "Detective Killer" are all connected to Siesta. She is a constant presence in absence.

Feat 13: Strategic Pre‑Planning – Preparing Charlotte for the Future

Siesta's letter, her training of Charlotte, and her decision to leave behind a legacy all demonstrate strategic pre‑planning. She knew she might die and prepared Charlotte to continue her work. This is a long‑term strategy that spans beyond her own lifetime.

Premises:

1. Mortality Awareness Premise: Siesta knew she was in danger (she was being hunted by the Detective Killer).
2. Charlotte's Training Premise: She trained Charlotte as a disciple.
3. Legacy Premise: She left behind clues and goals for Charlotte.
4. Letter Premise: She wrote a final letter.
5. Succession Premise: She identified Charlotte as her successor.

Chain of Reasoning (inferred):

* Step 1: Recognize that her life is in danger.
* Step 2: Identify Charlotte as her successor – someone with potential.
* Step 3: Train Charlotte – give her the skills she needs.
* Step 4: Leave a letter with a mission – defeat the Detective Killer.
* Step 5: Trust that Charlotte will succeed.
* Step 6: This ensures her legacy continues after her death.

Conclusion: Siesta prepared for her death by grooming Charlotte as her successor and giving her a mission. This is a testament to her long‑term strategic thinking.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

(Prologue): "Please protect the order of the world. I'm counting on you, Charl."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Long‑term Planning: S (Prepares for her own death and succession.)
Strategic Training: A (Grooms Charlotte as a successor.)
Trust in Others: A (Believes in Charlotte's ability.)
Legacy Management: S (Ensures her will continues after death.)
Succession Planning: S (Identifies and grooms a successor.)

Feat 14: The "Detective Killer" – Siesta's Role in Identifying a Threat

Siesta's letter identifies the "Detective Killer" as the one who vanquished her. This suggests that before her death, Siesta had identified a specific threat to detectives – a person or group targeting them. While the letter is a mislead, it correctly identifies that there is a threat. This shows Siesta was investigating this threat before her death.

Premises:

1. Threat Identification Premise: Siesta identified the "Detective Killer" before her death.
2. Letter Premise: She passed this information to Charlotte.
3. Mislead Premise: The letter is incomplete but directionally correct.
4. Investigation Premise: Siesta had been investigating the threat.
5. Risk Awareness Premise: She knew the threat was serious enough to mention.

Chain of Reasoning (inferred):

* Step 1: Investigate the threat to detectives – notice a pattern of detective deaths.
* Step 2: Identify the "Detective Killer" – a person or group targeting detectives.
* Step 3: Document this in her letter.
* Step 4: Trust Charlotte to investigate further.
* Step 5: This sets Charlotte on the right path.

Conclusion: Siesta identified the threat and passed the information to Charlotte, enabling her to continue the investigation.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

"I want you to defeat the 'Detective Killer' who vanquished me."

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Threat Identification: A (Identifies the "Detective Killer.")
Information Transfer: A (Passes information to Charlotte.)
Investigation: A (Had been investigating the threat.)
Risk Awareness: A (Recognizes the seriousness of the threat.)

Feat 15: The Empty Musket – Symbolic Victory

In the daydream, Siesta's musket is empty – she has no bullets. This is symbolic of her victory without violence. She wins through prediction and psychological insight, not force. This embodies her philosophy that a first‑rate detective resolves incidents before they occur.

Premises:

1. Empty Musket Premise: The musket is empty – no bullets.
2. Symbolism Premise: Represents victory without violence.
3. Prediction Premise: She predicted Charlotte's actions.
4. Philosophy Premise: This embodies her catchphrase.
5. Psychological Victory Premise: She wins by understanding Charlotte's mind.

Chain of Reasoning:

* Step 1: Know that Charlotte will hesitate to shoot.
* Step 2: Use the empty musket as a symbol – she doesn't need bullets.
* Step 3: Touch Charlotte's pendant – a symbolic "defeat."
* Step 4: This demonstrates her victory through prediction.

Conclusion: The empty musket symbolizes Siesta's victory through psychological insight and prediction, embodying her philosophy.

Key Dialogue (paraphrased):

(Siesta touches her gun's barrel to Charlotte's pendant.)

(In the end, there was no gunshot. The musket was empty.)

SCD Categories & Tier Ratings:

Symbolic Communication: A (Uses the empty musket as a symbol.)
Psychological Dominance: S (Wins without firing a shot.)
Predictive Ability: S (Predicted Charlotte's actions.)
Philosophical Consistency: S (Embodies her catchphrase.)

Overall Cognitive Tier for Code: RED: S (Genius) – Though Siesta is dead for most of the volume, her cognitive feats are demonstrated through her pre‑death actions: resolving the hijacking (pre‑emptive planning, crisis management, combat proficiency, information control), training Charlotte (mentorship, strategic training, relationship building), writing the letter (psychological insight, emotional communication, strategic misdirection), and identifying the Detective Killer (threat identification, investigation). The daydream sequence shows her psychological insight and ability to confront Charlotte's fears, while also demonstrating meta‑awareness and predictive ability. Her legacy drives the entire plot, demonstrating long‑term strategic planning and succession management. The empty musket scene is particularly notable – it shows Siesta winning without firing a shot, purely through prediction and psychological dominance, perfectly embodying her catchphrase: "A first‑rate detective resolves incidents before they even occur." Her influence extends beyond her death, making her a constant presence in Charlotte's journey.
